			$0\in S$, after shrinking $S$ to a coordinate neighborhood of $0$,
			the restriction map
			\[
			H^q\!\left(X,\mathcal O_X(K_X+L)\right)\longrightarrow
			H^q\!\left(X_0,\mathcal O_{X_0}(K_X+L)\right)
			\]
			is surjective for every $q\geq0$.
		\end{itemize}
<!-- end theorem-19 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
Part~\hyperref[Exthigherdeg-part-1]{(1)} is the injectivity theorem of
		\cite[Theorem~1.1]{CDM17} and
		\cite[Theorem~1.1]{ZZ22}. Indeed, tensoring
		the short exact sequence
		\[
		0\longrightarrow\mathcal I(he^{-\psi})
		\longrightarrow\mathcal I(h)
		\longrightarrow
		\frac{\mathcal I(h)}{\mathcal I(he^{-\psi})}
		\longrightarrow0
		\]
		with $\mathcal O_X(K_X+L)$ and taking cohomology gives a connecting map from
		the quotient cohomology in degree $q$ to
		$H^{q+1}\!\left(X,\mathcal O_X(K_X+L)\otimes
		\mathcal I(he^{-\psi})\right)$. The injectivity
		assertion in degree $q+1$ forces this connecting map to vanish, and hence
		the quotient map in degree $q$ is surjective.
		Part~\hyperref[Exthigherdeg-part-2]{(2)} is
		\cite[Corollary~1.5]{Matsumura22}.
		
		For part~\hyperref[Exthigherdeg-part-3]{(3)}, let $t$ be a local coordinate on $S$ centered at $0$.
		Since $f$ is proper and the coordinate neighborhood is Stein, its
		preimage is holomorphically convex. Set $s=f^*t$ and take
		$\psi=\log|s|^2$. Since $h$ is smooth,
		\[
		\mathcal I(h)=\mathcal O_X,
		\qquad
		\mathcal I(he^{-\psi})=\mathcal I_{X_0}
		\]
		by \cite[Remark~5.9]{Dem12}. The Poincar\'e--Lelong formula gives
		$\sqrt{-1}\partial\bar\partial\psi=[X_0]\geq0$, so the curvature
		hypothesis in part~\hyperref[Exthigherdeg-part-1]{(1)} is satisfied. Its surjective formulation now
		gives precisely the asserted restriction map.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- corollary-4: body_md -->
\label{c-higher-plurigenera}
		Let $f:X\to S$ be a smooth family with $X$ K\"ahler. If $K_X$ is semipositive, then, for every $m\geq 1$ and $q\geq 0$, the higher cohomological plurigenus
		\[
		P_m^{(q)}(X_t)=\dim H^q(X_t,mK_{X_t})
		\]
		is independent of $t\in S$.
<!-- end corollary-4 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
Work near an arbitrary point $0\in S$. Applying Theorem~\ref{Exthigherdeg} to $L=(m-1)K_X$, equipped with the induced semipositive metric, shows that the restriction maps in every degree are surjective. Theorem~\ref{l-Extension} therefore gives local constancy, and connectedness of $S$ gives constancy on the entire base.
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- theorem-20: body_md -->
\label{t-ginvarpluri}
		Let $f:X\to S$ be a proper Moishezon morphism with connected fibers over a relatively compact, smooth connected curve $S$ and
		$(X,\mathbf M)$ a generalized pair with abundant nef part over $S$. Write
		\[
		K_{X_t}+\mathbf M_t
		=\left.(K_X+\mathbf M_X)\right|_{X_t},
		\]
		and assume that $(X_t,\mathbf M_t)$ has generalized canonical
		singularities for every $t\in S$. Then, for every $m\geq1$ such that
		$m\mathbf M_X$ is Cartier, the generalized $m$-th plurigenera
		\[
		t\longmapsto
		\dim H^0\!\left(X_t,
		\mathcal O_{X_t}\bigl(m(K_{X_t}+\mathbf M_t)\bigr)\right)
		\]
		is constant on $S$.
<!-- end theorem-20 -->
<!-- definition-16: body_md -->
\label{d-abundant}
		Let $p:X\to S$ be a proper surjective morphism whose general fiber lies
		in Fujiki's class $\mathcal C$. A relatively nef $\mathbb Q$-Cartier
		divisor $L$ is \emph{abundant} (or \emph{good}) over $S$ if
		$\nu(L/S)=\kappa(L/S)$, where $\kappa(\cdot)$ is the Kodaira dimension and $\nu(\cdot)$ is the numerical Kodaira dimension. Recall that, for a nef $\mathbb Q$-Cartier
		divisor $A$ on a compact complex variety, its \emph{numerical Kodaira dimension}
		is defined by
		\[
		\nu(A):=\max\left\{\ell\geq0\;\middle|\; A^\ell\not\equiv0\right\}.
		\]
		For an analytically sufficiently general point $t\in S$, set
		\[
		\nu(L/S):=\nu\!\left(L|_{X_t}\right),
		\]
		where $X_t=p^{-1}(t)$.
<!-- end definition-16 -->
<!-- proposition-11: body_md -->
\label{p-nefabundant}
		Let $f:X\to S$ be a proper Moishezon morphism, and let $L$ be a relatively nef and abundant
		$\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor on $X$ (Definition~\ref{d-abundant}). For every
		$s\in S$, there exists an open neighborhood $U\subset S$ of $s$ such that,
		writing $X_U:=f^{-1}(U)$, there are a modification
		$\mu:X'\to X_U$, a proper contraction $g:X'\to Z$, and a projective
		morphism $h:Z\to U$ fitting into a commutative diagram
		\[
		\begin{tikzcd}
			X' \arrow[r,"\mu"] \arrow[d,"g"'] & X_U \arrow[d,"f|_{X_U}"] \\
			Z \arrow[r,"h"'] & U,
		\end{tikzcd}
		\]
		such that
		\[
		\mu^*\!\left(L|_{X_U}\right)\sim_{\mathbb Q,U}g^*H
		\]
		for some $h$-nef and $h$-big $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $H$ on $Z$.
<!-- end proposition-11 -->
<!-- definition-17: body_md -->
\label{def-nefabun}
		A $\mathbb Q$-divisorial generalized pair $(X/S,B+\mathbf M)$, with
		structural morphism $f:X\to S$, has an \emph{abundant nef part} if, for
		every $s\in S$, there exists an open neighborhood $U\subset S$ of $s$
		such that, writing $X_U:=f^{-1}(U)$, there are a modification
		$\mu:X'\to X_U$ to which $\mathbf M|_{X_U}$ descends and a factorization
		of $f|_{X_U}\circ\mu$
		\[
		X'\xrightarrow{g}Z\xrightarrow{h}U
		\]
		such that
		\[
		\mathbf M_{X'}\sim_{\mathbb Q,U}g^*H
		\]
		for some $h$-nef and $h$-big $\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $H$ on $Z$, and $h:Z\to U$ is projective.
<!-- end definition-17 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{l-bignef}
		In the setting of Definition~\ref{def-nefabun}, fix $0\in S$. After
		shrinking $S$ around $0$, choose $\mu:X'\to X$ and a factorization
		$X'\xrightarrow{g}Z\xrightarrow{h}S$ as in that definition, and denote by
		$\widetilde X_0$ the strict transform of $X_0$ on $X'$. After replacing
		$X'$ by a higher model and shrinking $S$ further, there are effective
		$\mathbb Q$-divisors $E,F$ on $X'$ without common components, with $E$
		$\mu$-exceptional and $\widetilde X_0\not\subset\operatorname{Supp}(E+F)$,
		such that, for every $k\geq1$, there is an $h$-ample
		$\mathbb Q$-Cartier divisor $H_k$ satisfying
		\[
		\mathbf M_{X'}+\frac1kE
		\sim_{\mathbb Q,S}g^*H_k+\frac1kF.
		\]
		Moreover, this equivalence defines a metric $h_k'$ by global sections on
		$\mathbf M_{X'}+\frac1kE$ and a push-forward metric
		$h_k:=\mu_*h_k'$ on $\mathbf M_X$, both with nonnegative curvature
		current and with nontrivial restriction to the central fiber (we write $h_{k,0}:=h_k|_{X_0}$).
		If
		\[
		\mu^*\mathbf M_X=\mathbf M_{X'}+\Xi
		\]
		and $\varphi_k,\varphi_E,\varphi_F,\varphi_\Xi$ denote the corresponding
		local weights, let
		$\varphi_{k,0},\varphi_{E_0},\varphi_{F_0},\varphi_{\Xi_0}$ denote their
		restrictions to the central fiber. Then
		\[
		\mu^*\varphi_k+\frac1k\varphi_E
		\approx\frac1k\varphi_F+\varphi_\Xi,
		\qquad
		\mu_0^*\varphi_{k,0}+\frac1k\varphi_{E_0}
		\approx\frac1k\varphi_{F_0}+\varphi_{\Xi_0}.
		\]
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
By Proposition~\ref{p-nefabundant}, the intermediate morphism
		$h:Z\to S$ is projective, and
		$\mathbf M_{X'}\sim_{\mathbb Q,S}g^*H$ for an $h$-nef and $h$-big
		divisor $H$. By the relative Kodaira lemma, there is
		an effective $\mathbb Q$-divisor $G$ on $Z$ such that, for every
		$k\geq1$,
		\[
		H\sim_{\mathbb Q,S}H_k+\frac1kG
		\]
		with $H_k$ $h$-ample. Set
		\[
		c:=\operatorname{mult}_{X_0}\!\left(\mu_*g^*G\right),\qquad
		D:=g^*G-c(f\circ\mu)^*(0).
		\]
		The divisor $D$ does not contain $\widetilde X_0$, and every component of
		its negative part is $\mu$-exceptional. As in
		\cite[Lemma~4.2]{LW26}, write $D=F-E$ as the difference of its positive and
		negative parts. Then $E$ and $F$ are effective divisors without common
		components, $E$ is $\mu$-exceptional, and
		$\widetilde X_0\not\subset\operatorname{Supp}(E+F)$. Since
		$(f\circ\mu)^*(0)$ is trivial over $S$, we obtain
		\[
		\mathbf M_{X'}+\frac1kE
		\sim_{\mathbb Q,S}g^*H_k+\frac1kF.
		\]
		This is the construction in \cite[Lemma~4.2]{LW26}. 
		
		After shrinking $S$, choose a sufficiently divisible integer $r$ such that
		$rH_k$ is Cartier and its relative complete linear system defines an
		embedding
		\[
		\iota_k:Z\hookrightarrow\mathbb P^N_S.
		\]
		Choose relative global sections
		$\sigma_0,\ldots,\sigma_N\in H^0(Z,\mathcal O_Z(rH_k))$ with no common
		zero that define the embedding $\iota_k$. If
		$\theta:\mathcal O_Z(rH_k)|_U\simeq\mathcal O_U$ is
		a local trivialization, the pullback of the relative Fubini--Study metric has
		local weight
		\[
		\varphi_{rH_k}=\frac12\log\!\left(
		\sum_{j=0}^N|\theta(\sigma_j)|^2\right).
		\]
		The sections have no common zero, so this weight is smooth and
		plurisubharmonic. Taking the $r$-th root gives a smooth metric $h_{H_k}$ on
		$H_k$ with nonnegative curvature, and we set
		\[
		\chi_k:=g^*h_{H_k}.
		\]
		Thus $\chi_k$ is a smooth semipositive metric on $g^*H_k$ defined by global
		sections. Twisting it by the canonical metric $\bar{h}_F$ on $F$ gives the metric
		\[
		h_k':=\chi_k\,\overline h_F^{1/k}
		\]
		on $g^*H_k+\frac1kF$, and therefore, via the equivalence above, on
		$\mathbf M_{X'}+\frac1kE$. Because $\widetilde X_0$ is not a component
		of $F$, the restriction of $h_k'$ to $\widetilde X_0$ is not identically infinite,
		and
		\[
		\varphi_k'\approx\frac1k\varphi_F,\quad
		\varphi_{k,0}'\approx\frac1k\varphi_{F_0}.
		\]
		Finally, the negativity lemma gives
		$\mu^*\mathbf M_X=\mathbf M_{X'}+\Xi$ with $\Xi\geq0$
		$\mu$-exceptional. Since $h_k'$ is defined by global sections, its
		push-forward $h_k:=\mu_*h_k'$ is a well-defined metric on $\mathbf M_X$.
		To compare the two metrics, observe that $h_k'$ is a metric on
		$\mathbf M_{X'}+\frac1kE$, while
		\[
		\mu^*\mathbf M_X
		=\mathbf M_{X'}+\Xi
		=\left(\mathbf M_{X'}+\frac1kE\right)
		+\left(\Xi-\frac1kE\right).
		\]
		Hence the difference between the two underlying divisors is
		$\Xi-\frac1kE$. By the definition of the push-forward metric and the
		multiplicativity of divisorial metrics,
		\[
		\mu^*h_k
		=h_k'\,\overline h_\Xi\,\overline h_E^{-1/k}
		\]
		up to multiplication by a positive locally bounded function. Equivalently,
		\[
		\mu^*h_k\cdot\overline h_E^{1/k}
		=h_k'\,\overline h_\Xi
		\]
		up to the same type of factor. Passing to local weights, we obtain
		\[
		\mu^*\varphi_k+\frac1k\varphi_E
		\approx\varphi_k'+\varphi_\Xi
		\approx\frac1k\varphi_F+\varphi_\Xi.
		\]
		Restricting this identity to the central fiber similarly gives
		\[
		\mu_0^*\varphi_{k,0}+\frac1k\varphi_{E_0}
		\approx\varphi_{k,0}'+\varphi_{\Xi_0}
		\approx\frac1k\varphi_{F_0}+\varphi_{\Xi_0}.
		\]
		These are precisely the asserted weight identities; the curvature currents
		are nonnegative because all the metrics involved are defined by global
		sections, as in \cite[Lemmas~4.3 and~4.4]{LW26}. Thus the only new input
		in the Moishezon setting is the projectivity of the auxiliary morphism
		$Z\to S$ supplied by Proposition~\ref{p-nefabundant}; the remainder of
		the metric construction is unchanged.
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- proposition-12: body_md -->
\label{t-LWext}
		Let $\pi:X\to S$ be a Moishezon family with connected fibers over a
		relatively compact, smooth, connected curve and $(L,h)$ a line bundle
		with a singular Hermitian metric of nonnegative curvature current, and
		assume that $X_0$ has canonical singularities and $h|_{X_0}$ is well
		defined. Then every section of
		\[
		H^0\!\left(
		X_0,\mathcal O_{X_0}(mK_{X_0}+L|_{X_0})
		\otimes\mathcal G_m(h|_{X_0})\right)
		\]
		extends over a neighborhood of $X_0$.
<!-- end proposition-12 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
After shrinking $S$ around $0$, we fix
		\[
		s\in H^0\!\left(X_0,
		\mathcal O_{X_0}(mK_{X_0}+L|_{X_0})
		\otimes\mathcal G_m(h|_{X_0})\right).
		\]
		By \cite[Lemma~5.12]{LW26}, there is a resolution
		$\nu_0:\widetilde X_0\to X_0$ such that membership in
		$\mathcal G_m(h|_{X_0})$ is equivalent to the ordinary multiplier ideal
		condition:
		\[
		\nu_0^*s\in H^0\!\left(\widetilde X_0,
		\mathcal O_{\widetilde X_0}
		\bigl(mK_{\widetilde X_0}+\nu_0^*(L|_{X_0})\bigr)
		\otimes\mathcal I\bigl(\nu_0^*(h|_{X_0})\bigr)\right).
		\]
		Then, by \cite[Lemma~5.13]{LW26}, there are smooth complex manifold $Y_0\subset Y$, a
		resolution $\mu:Y\to X$, and a morphism
		$\tau_0:Y_0\to\widetilde X_0$ such that
		\[
		\mu_0:=\mu|_{Y_0}=\nu_0\circ\tau_0.
		\]
		By \cite[Lemma~5.11]{LW26}, the multiplier ideal condition is preserved
		after passing to this higher resolution; hence
		\[
		\mu_0^*s\in H^0\!\left(Y_0,
		\mathcal O_{Y_0}\bigl(mK_{Y_0}+\mu_0^*(L|_{X_0})\bigr)
		\otimes\mathcal I\bigl(\mu_0^*(h|_{X_0})\bigr)\right).
		\]
		
		Because $X\to S$ is Moishezon, we may first take a projective modification
		and then choose $\mu$ projective over $S$. Therefore
		Takayama's extension theorem \cite[Theorem~3.1]{Taka06} (and also \cite[Theorem 5.1]{LW26} in the twisted setting) extends
		$\mu_0^*s$ from $Y_0$ to $Y$. After a
		further shrinking of $S$, the total space $X$ has canonical singularities
		\cite{KawaDeform99}. Put $L_0:=L|_{X_0}$. The restriction and pullback fit
		into the commutative diagram
		\[
		\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large, row sep=large]
			H^0\!\left(Y,\mathcal O_Y(mK_Y+\mu^*L)\right)
			\arrow[r,"\mathrm{res}"]
			& H^0\!\left(Y_0,
			\mathcal O_{Y_0}(mK_Y|_{Y_0}+\mu_0^*L_0)\right) \\
			H^0\!\left(X,\mathcal O_X(mK_X+L)\right)
			\arrow[u,"\mu^*"']
			\arrow[r,"\mathrm{res}"']
			& H^0\!\left(X_0,
			\mathcal O_{X_0}(mK_{X_0}+L_0)\right)
			\arrow[u,hook].
		\end{tikzcd}
		\]
		The left vertical map is an isomorphism because $X$ has canonical
		singularities. To describe the right vertical map, write
		\[
		K_{Y_0}=\mu_0^*K_{X_0}+A_0,
		\]
		where $A_0\geq0$ is $\mu_0$-exceptional because $X_0$ has canonical
		singularities. Moreover, if
		$\mu^*X_0=Y_0+\Theta$ and $\Theta_0:=\Theta|_{Y_0}$, then adjunction gives
		\[
		K_Y|_{Y_0}\sim K_{Y_0}+\Theta_0,
		\qquad \Theta_0\geq0.
		\]
		The right vertical arrow is therefore the composition
		\[
		\begin{aligned}
			H^0\!\left(X_0,\mathcal O_{X_0}(mK_{X_0}+L_0)\right)
			&\xrightarrow{\ \mu_0^*\ }
			H^0\!\left(Y_0,
			\mathcal O_{Y_0}(m\mu_0^*K_{X_0}+\mu_0^*L_0)\right)\\
			&\hookrightarrow
			H^0\!\left(Y_0,
			\mathcal O_{Y_0}(mK_{Y_0}+\mu_0^*L_0)\right)\\
			&\hookrightarrow
			H^0\!\left(Y_0,
			\mathcal O_{Y_0}(mK_Y|_{Y_0}+\mu_0^*L_0)\right),
		\end{aligned}
		\]
		where first map is injective because $\mu_0$ is birational, and the next two maps are inclusions
		induced by the effective divisors $mA_0$ and $m\Theta_0$, respectively.
		Hence the extension of $\mu_0^*s$ on $Y$ corresponds to a section on $X$
		whose restriction is $s$.
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- proposition-13: body_md -->
\label{t-gpairinvPluri}
		Let $\pi:X\to S$ be as in Theorem~\ref{t-ginvarpluri} and
		$(X,\mathbf M)$ the generalized pair there, and fix $0\in S$. For
		every $m\geq1$ such
		that $m\mathbf M_X$ is Cartier, after shrinking $S$ around $0$, the
		restriction map
		\[
		H^0\!\left(X,
		\mathcal O_X\bigl(m(K_X+\mathbf M_X)\bigr)\right)
		\longrightarrow
		H^0\!\left(X_0,
		\mathcal O_{X_0}\bigl(m(K_{X_0}+\mathbf M_0)\bigr)\right)
		\]
		is surjective.
<!-- end proposition-13 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
After shrinking $S$, we may assume that $X$ has
		canonical singularities \cite{KawaDeform99}. Choose a model
		$\mu:X'\to X$ on which $\mathbf M$ descends and for which
		Proposition~\ref{p-nefabundant} and Lemma~\ref{l-bignef} apply. Define the discrepancy divisors
		$A_0^{\mathrm{gen}}$ and $A_0$ by
		\[
		K_{\widetilde X_0}+\mathbf M_{X'}|_{\widetilde X_0}
		=\mu_0^*(K_{X_0}+\mathbf M_0)+A_0^{\mathrm{gen}},
		\qquad
		K_{\widetilde X_0}=\mu_0^*K_{X_0}+A_0.
		\]
		Generalized canonicity gives $A_0^{\mathrm{gen}}\geq0$. Moreover, since $X_0$ has canonical singularities, $A_0\geq0$. Write
		\[
		\mu^*\mathbf M_X=\mathbf M_{X'}+\Xi,
		\qquad \Xi\geq0,
		\]
		where $\Xi$ is $\mu$-exceptional, and set
		$\Xi_0:=\Xi|_{\widetilde X_0}$. Thus
		$\mu_0^*\mathbf M_0=\mathbf M_{X'}|_{\widetilde X_0}+\Xi_0$, and comparison
		of the two discrepancy formulas gives
		\[
		A_0^{\mathrm{gen}}=A_0-\Xi_0.
		\]
		
		Fix $m\ge 1$ and a section
		$s\in H^0(X_0,\mathcal{O}_{X_0}(m(K_{X_0}+\mathbf M_0)))$. To apply
		Proposition~\ref{t-LWext}, we must show that, for $k\gg1$,
		\[
		s\in H^0\!\left(X_0,
		\mathcal O_{X_0}\bigl(m(K_{X_0}+\mathbf M_0)\bigr)
		\otimes\mathcal G_m(h_{k,0}^m)\right).
		\]
		If $\alpha$ denotes the local coefficient of $s$ in a trivialization,
		then the definition of $\mathcal G_m$ and \cite[Lemma~5.12]{LW26} reduce
		this membership to the local integrability condition
		\[
		\int_{\mu_0^{-1}(U)}
		\|\mu_0^*\alpha\|^2_{\mu_0^*(h_{k,0}^m)}
		|J(\mu_0)|^{2m}\,dV_{\widetilde X_0}<\infty\tag{*}
		\]
		for every sufficiently small open set. Note that
		$\operatorname{div}(J(\mu_0))
		=K_{\widetilde X_0}-\mu_0^*K_{X_0}=A_0$. The weight identities in
		Lemma~\ref{l-bignef} give
		\[
		-m\mu_0^*\varphi_{k,0}+m\varphi_{A_0}
		\approx
		m\left(-\frac1k\varphi_{F_0}
		+\frac1k\varphi_{E_0}+\varphi_{A_0^{\mathrm{gen}}}\right).
		\]
		Since $A_0^{\mathrm{gen}}$ and $E_0$ are effective, Skoda's integrability
		lemma (cf. \cite[Lemma 5.6]{Dem12}) shows that the integral $(*)$ is finite once $k$ is chosen so that
		\[
		\frac{2m}{k}\,
		\nu\!\left(\Theta_{\overline h_{F_0}},y\right)<1
		\quad\text{for every }y\in\widetilde X_0.
		\]
		Consequently, every section $s$ as above vanishing along
		$\mathcal G_m(h_{k,0}^m)$ for $k\gg1$.
		
		We now equip $m \mathbf{M}_X$ with the metric $h_k^m$. Proposition~\ref{t-LWext} then extends every section from the
		central fiber, which completes the proof.
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
Apply Proposition~\ref{t-gpairinvPluri} near an arbitrary point of $S$. Thus
		every generalized pluricanonical section on a fiber extends locally over
		the base. The extension criterion in Theorem~\ref{l-Extension} gives the
		local constancy of the corresponding $h^0$ (see the proof of \cite[Corollary~1.3]{LW26}), and connectedness of $S$
		gives constancy on the whole base.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- proposition-14: body_md -->
Let $f:X \to Y$ be a proper morphism of complex spaces where $Y$ is a reduced and smooth complex space of dimension one, $\mathscr{F}$ a coherent analytic sheaf on $X$ which is flat with respect to $f$, $y$ a point in $Y$, and $q$ an integer. Then $h^q(X_y,\mathscr{F}_{(y)})$ is locally constant if and only if $R^{q+1}f_*\mathscr{F}$ and $R^{q}f_*\mathscr{F}$ are torsion free.
<!-- end proposition-14 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
Fix a point $y\in Y$. Since the argument is local on $Y$, we may replace $Y$ by a coordinate neighborhood of $y$ and $X$ by its inverse image. Let $t$ be a local coordinate with $t(y)=0$, and denote its pullback to $X$ by the same symbol. Flatness of $\mathscr{F}$ over $Y$ implies that multiplication by $t$ on $\mathscr{F}$ is injective and hence defines an isomorphism $\mathscr{F}\xrightarrow{\sim}t\mathscr{F}$. The inclusion $\iota:t\mathscr{F}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{F}$ fits into the short exact sequence
		$$
		0 \to t\mathscr{F} \xrightarrow{\iota} \mathscr{F} \to \frac{\mathscr{F}}{t\mathscr{F}} \to 0.
		$$
		Applying the higher direct image functors gives the long exact sequence
		$$
		\cdots \to R^qf_*\mathscr{F} \xrightarrow{\gamma_q} R^qf_*(\frac{\mathscr{F}}{t\mathscr{F}}) \xrightarrow{\delta_q} R^{q+1}f_*(t\mathscr{F}) \xrightarrow{\iota_{q+1}} R^{q+1}f_*\mathscr{F} \to \cdots.
		$$
		Here $\iota_k:=R^kf_*(\iota)$. Let
		$\nu:t\mathscr{F}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathscr{F}$ be the inverse of the
		multiplication isomorphism, given by $ts\mapsto s$. For each $k$, it induces
		an isomorphism
		\[
		\jmath_k:=R^kf_*(\nu):R^kf_*(t\mathscr{F})
		\xrightarrow{\sim}R^kf_*\mathscr{F}.
		\]
		Since $\iota=(\cdot t)\circ\nu$, functoriality gives
		\[
		\iota_k=(\cdot t)\circ\jmath_k.
		\]
		Thus $\iota_k$ factors through $\jmath_k$, followed by multiplication by
		$t$ on $R^kf_*\mathscr{F}$.
		
		For the ``if'' direction, assume that $R^{q+1}f_*\mathscr{F}$ and
		$R^qf_*\mathscr{F}$ are torsion free. The factorization
		\[
		\iota_{q+1}=(\cdot t)\circ\jmath_{q+1}
		\]
		shows that $\iota_{q+1}$ is injective, since $\jmath_{q+1}$ is an
		isomorphism and multiplication by $t$ on $R^{q+1}f_*\mathscr{F}$ is
		injective by the torsion freeness of $R^{q+1}f_*\mathscr{F}$. Exactness gives $\delta_q=0$, so $\gamma_q$ is surjective.
		The preceding map in the long exact sequence is $\iota_q$; since
		$\jmath_q$ is an isomorphism, its image is $t\cdot R^qf_*\mathscr{F}$. Hence
		\[
		\ker(\gamma_q)=t\cdot R^qf_*\mathscr{F},
		\]
		and we obtain an isomorphism
		\[
		\frac{R^qf_*\mathscr{F}}{t\cdot R^qf_*\mathscr{F}}
		\cong R^qf_*\bigl(\mathscr{F}/t\mathscr{F}\bigr).
		\]
		Since $\mathscr{F}/t\mathscr{F}$ is the direct image of
		$\mathscr{F}_{(y)}$ under the inclusion $X_y\hookrightarrow X$,
		Proposition \ref{higher stalk} yields
		\[
		\bigl(R^qf_*(\mathscr{F}/t\mathscr{F})\bigr)_y
		\cong H^q(X_y,\mathscr{F}_{(y)}).
		\]
		Taking stalks at $y$, we therefore obtain
		\[
		\frac{(R^qf_*\mathscr{F})_y}{t\cdot(R^qf_*\mathscr{F})_y}
		\cong H^q(X_y,\mathscr{F}_{(y)}).
		\]
		The left-hand side is the fiber of $R^qf_*\mathscr{F}$ at $y$.
		By Theorem \ref{direct image}, $R^qf_*\mathscr{F}$ is coherent;
		since it is torsion free on the smooth curve $Y$, it is locally free.
		As $y$ was arbitrary, the function
		$y\mapsto h^q(X_y,\mathscr{F}_{(y)})$ equals its locally constant rank
		function and is therefore locally constant.
		
		For the ``only if'' direction, assume that
		$z\mapsto h^q(X_z,\mathscr{F}_{(z)})$ is locally constant.
		By Grauert's theorem on cohomology and base change,
		$R^qf_*\mathscr{F}$ is locally free, hence torsion free, and the base
		change map at $y$ is an isomorphism. Under the identification above,
		this implies that $(\gamma_q)_y$ is surjective. Exactness then gives
		$(\delta_q)_y=0$, so $(\iota_{q+1})_y$ is injective.
		Suppose that $(R^{q+1}f_*\mathscr{F})_y$ has a nonzero torsion element.
		Since $\mathcal{O}_{Y,y}$ is a discrete valuation ring with uniformizer
		$t$, there exist a nonzero element $u$ and a least integer $n\geq1$
		such that $t^n\cdot u=0$. Setting $s=t^{n-1}\cdot u$, we have $s\neq0$ and $t\cdot s=0$.
		The isomorphism $(\jmath_{q+1})_y$ gives a nonzero element
		\[
		x=(\jmath_{q+1})_y^{-1}(s)
		\in\bigl(R^{q+1}f_*(t\mathscr{F})\bigr)_y.
		\]
		The factorization above yields
		\[
		(\iota_{q+1})_y(x)=t\cdot(\jmath_{q+1})_y(x)=t\cdot s=0,
		\]
		contradicting the injectivity of $(\iota_{q+1})_y$.
		As $y$ was arbitrary, $R^{q+1}f_*\mathscr{F}$ is torsion free.
<!-- end proof-24 -->
